制造业PMI 非制造业PMI
10月份制造业PMI季节性回落至收缩区间（49.3）。分项来看，价格分项高位回落但仍维持扩
张，购进和出厂价格分别为  56.8（-4.0）和51.7（-2.3）  ，二者差缩至  5.1pcts  ，中下游利润挤
压边际缓解。生产指数  49.8（-1.9）  、新订单  49.4（-1.1）  ，产需缺口由上月1.2pcts收窄
至  0.4pcts  ，两者均落入收缩区间，指向企业根据订单变化主动下调产出节奏。新出口订单回落

至  48.7（-1.3）  ，不过仍高于过去五年同期水平，显示外需支撑边际减弱但仍具有相对韧性。原
材料库存  48.4（+0.2）  、产成品库存  47.5（-0.1）  ，叠加采购量  48.9  （-2.1）与进口  47.0（-
2.2）  回落，反映订单走弱背景下企业“以销定产”。 高技术  51.3  与装备  50.6  维持扩张，对冲总
量回落，而消费品  48.8  、高耗能  47.3  拖累更加明显，“K型分化”扩大。大型企业  50.2  相对稳
定，中小企业收缩进一步深化，分化加剧。非制造业商务活动指数  49.7（-0.5）  ，服务业  50.0
（-0.2）  ，建筑业  48.0（-2.3）未能延续9月的回升态势，“银十”未形成有效托底，建筑开工
进度偏慢。总体来看，10月回落幅度与历史季节性大致相当，短期扰动（长假与前期备货透支）
叠加需求接续不足是主因，四季度修复仍依赖财政资金加快转化为实物工作量与内需改善落地。

Document 026: 2026-10-30_［QE予測］7～9月期実質GDPは年率＋1.0％とプラス成長を予想（1次QE）_4f5741cf

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026-10-30_［QE予測］7～9月期実質GDPは年率＋1.0％とプラス成長を予想（1次QE）_4f5741cf.pdf
Report source: Mizuho
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

1

7～9 月期実質GDP は年率＋1.0％と 
プラス成長を予想（1 次QE）

○ 2026年7～9月期の実質GDP（1次速報値）は前期比＋0.2％（年率＋1.0％）と、4～6月期（前期比＋
0.4％）からやや減速するものの、4四半期連続でのプラス成長を予想 
○ 個人消費が伸び悩む一方、設備投資や民間在庫が押し上げる見込み。なお、公的在庫と輸入では
中東情勢を主因とする一時的な変動が生じ、成長率にプラス・マイナス両面で影響 
○ 年度後半の日本経済は、小幅なプラス成長を予想。インフレ再加速を受け消費抑制が続く一方、設
備投資が堅調に拡大。日本銀行は12月会合で政策金利を1.50％に引き上げる見込み

7～9月期の実質GDP（1次
速報値）はプラス成長 を
予想 
11月16日 （月） に、 内閣府から2026年7～9月期の 「四半期別GDP速 報（QE）」
（1次速報値）が公表される。現時点で入手可能な基礎統計の結果を踏まえ
ると、7～9月期の実質GDPは前期比＋0.2％（ 年 率＋1.0％） となる見込みだ。
4～6月期の前期比＋0.4％（年率＋1.4％）からやや減速するものの、4四半
期連続でプラス成長になると予想する（図表）。 
7～9月期は、内需の柱である個人消費が伸び悩む一方、 設備投資の拡大や、
製造業を中心とする在庫積み増しが成長率を押し上げるとみられる。その他
では、前期の備蓄原油放出の反動が公的在庫投資のプラス要因として現れる
が、原油の代替調達進展を反映した輸入の増加がマイナス要因になるなど、 
図表 2026 年7～9 月期GDP（1 次速報）予測

(注) 図の項目別寄与度の合計は、誤差により実質GDP に完全に一致しない。表の値は、言及がない限り実質前期比（％） 、括弧内は実質GDP
前期比に対する寄与度（％Pt） 
(出所) 内閣府「四半期別GDP 速報」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 2

2

中東情勢による一時的な変動も引き続き成長率に影響する見込みだ。以下、
需要項目別に7～9月期の経済動向を詳しく確認しよう。 
個人消費は伸び悩み 。耐
久財の特需剥落や、 根強
い節約志向が影響 
個人消費は前期比▲0.1％（4～6月期：同＋0.0％） と、2四半期連続で伸び
悩む結果になろう。消費の弱さの背景には、 複合的な要因があると考えられ
る。 
耐久財では、自動車とエアコンの「特需」剥落が押し下げ要因になろう。
自動車は、購入時にかかる自動車税の環境性能割が3月末に廃止されたこと
を受け、4月から自動車販売が過去平均に比べ上振れていたが、8月以降は再
び過去平均並みの水準に戻っている。エアコンは、2027年4月の省エネ基準
引き上げで販売価格が上昇するとの見方から駆け込み需要が生じていたが、
景気ウォッチャー調査のコメントなどをみると、8月に入った後は一服した
模様だ。9月は気温が低かったほか、長雨による客足の減少もエアコンなど
季節家電の売り上げに悪影響を及ぼしたとみられる。 
食品やエネルギー、 各種消耗品などの非耐久財では、物価高を背景とする
根強い節約志向により、 弱含みが続いているようだ。今回のインフレ局面が
本格化する前の2020年と比較すると、非耐久消費財の物価は足元までの約6
年半でおよそ1.3倍の水準に上昇した計算になる。こうした累積的な価格上
昇が、 消費者の体感物価の高まりを通じて節約志向を強め、 購入数量の抑制
につながっている可能性がある。 
サービス消費は、 イベント関連支出などで底堅さがみられる一方、日常的
な支出には物価高による節約志向が波及し、 サービスや価格帯、 顧客層によ
る二極化が進みつつあるとみられる。 また、8～9月には台風・長雨による旅
行・ 宿泊のキャンセルや外出控えの影響から、 レジャー関連を中心に消費が
伸び悩んだようだ。 
設備投 資は3四半期ぶり
に前期比プラス 
設備投資は前期比＋1.2％（4～6月 期：同 ▲0.9％） と、3四半期ぶりに前期
比プラスへ転じると予想する。 
直近の設備投資関連指標をみると、資本財の国内向け供給量を表す資本財
総供給指数（除く輸送機械）が7～8月に大きく上昇した。また第3次産業活
動指数では、ソフトウェア業の実質売上高が同じく7～8月に増加したことが
確認でき、どちらも7～9月期の設備投資が拡大したことを示唆している。 
加えて、前期の4～6月期に発生した一時的な押し下げ圧力（特許権の海外
譲渡による国内研究開発投資の減少） がなくなったこともプラス要因だ。た
だし、 これは裏を返せば、7～9月期のQEにおける設備投資の伸びが実態以上
に強い数字になるということでもあり、評価する際には留意が必要である。 
住宅投資は 前期比マイナ
スが継続 
住宅投資は前期比▲0.8％（4～6月 期：同 ▲0.6％ ）と、2四半期連続で前期
比マイナスとなろう。中東情勢を背景とする資材不足は徐々に緩和している
とみられるが、7～8月の住宅着工件数が伸び悩んでおり、住宅価格や金利上

### Page 3

3

昇による需要低迷に加えて、 足元では地震や台風などによる着工 ・ 進捗の遅
れが発生している可能性がある。 
民間 在庫は 2四半期連続
で成長率にプラス寄与 
民間在庫変動の実質GDP成長率に対する寄与度は、＋0.2％Pt（4～6月期：
＋0.3％Pt）とプラスが続く見込みだ。AI関連需要の強さなどを背景に、製
造業で在庫積み増しの動きが進んでいることが影響すると考えられる。な
お、4～6月期は石油化学製品などの供給不足を懸念した前倒し発注が在庫を
押し上げたとみられるが、7～9月期は原油の代替調達進展に伴って供給不足
が緩和したことで、そうした要因の影響は一巡した模様だ。 
公的需要 は備蓄原油放出
の反動で前期比プラスに 
公的需要は前期比＋1.2％（4～6月 期：同 ▲0.8％）と、2四半期ぶりの前期
比プラスを予想する。 
最大の要因は、公的在庫変動の実質GDP成長率に対する寄与度がプラスに
転じるとみられることだ。4～6月期は国家備蓄原油の放出により公的在庫変
動の寄与度が▲0.5％Ptと大幅マイナスになったが、7～9月期は国家原油備
蓄の減少が小幅なものにとどまり（6月末：105日分→9月末：102日分）、公
的在庫の減少ペースが緩和することで、成長率に対し＋0.3％Pt程度のプラ
ス寄与になると見込まれる。 
その他の公的需要項目では、政府消費を前期比＋0.2％（4～6月期：同＋
1.7％）と予想する。4～6月期は高校授業料・小学校給食費の無償化開始に
より高い伸びになったが、7～9月期はそうした特殊要因が剥落し、緩やかな
増加ペースに戻ると考えられる。公共投資は前期比▲0.5％（4～6月期：同
▲0.5％）と、土木関連工事の減少を受けて2四半期連続のマイナスを見込む。 
輸出が小幅に減少する 一
方、 輸入が増加し、外需は
4四半期ぶりのマイナス
寄与 
外需では、輸出が前期比▲0.3％（4～6月期：同＋0.4％）と小幅に減少す
る一方、輸 入が同＋1.3％（4～6月 期：同▲1.7％）と増加に転じる見込みだ。
実質GDP成長率に対する外需の寄与度は▲0.3％Ptと、4四半期ぶりのマイナ
スが予想される。 
輸入の増加は、原油の代替調達進展が主因だ。中東情勢の緊迫化により4
～6月期は一時的に原油輸入が急減したが、7～9月期は米国産原油やホルム
ズ海峡を通過しない中東産原油の調達が増え、9月を除いて前年並みの輸入
量が確保されている。 
輸入はGDPの控除項目であることから、7～9月期は輸入増加が成長率の下
押し要因になる。 ただし、代替調達の進展に伴って国家備蓄原油の放出が抑
制され、 先述したように7～9月期は公的在庫変動が成長率にプラス寄与する
とみられることから、輸入増による成長率押し下げ効果の一部は公的在庫変
動により相殺されるだろう。 
輸出では、前期の4～6月期に特許権の海外譲渡がサービス輸出を押し上げ
た （これは4～6月期の設備投資の押し下げ要因でもある） 反動が現れ、 一時
的に下振れするだろう。一方、旺盛なAI関連需要が支えになり、半導体・電

### Page 4

4

子部品や半導体製造装置などの財輸出は堅調に推移する見込みだ。インバウ
ンド需要 （非居住者家計の国内での直接購入） は、 日中対立の継続による中
国人訪日客の少なさに加え、 航空運賃上昇を受けた欧米訪日客の伸び悩みに
より、低調な結果になると見込まれる。 
2026 年度後半 の実質 GDP
は小幅プラス と予想 。個
人消費が伸び悩む一方、
設備投資が堅調拡大 
以上のとおり、7～9月期の日本経済は前期から減速するものの、4四半期
連続でプラス成長になると予想する。GDPの約半分を占める個人消費の伸び
悩みが足を引っ張っているが、設備投資や製造業を中心とする在庫積み増し
がけん引する格好だ。 
先行きについてみると、2026年度後半（2026年10～2027年3月）の 実質GDP
は、 均してみれば小幅なプラス成長になると予想する。個人消費が振るわな
い一方で、 設備投資の拡大が支えになる構図が続くだろう。2026年度全体の
実質GDP成長率は、前年度末の堅調な伸びで今年度の平均水準が押し上げら
れることもあり（いわゆるプラスのゲタ）、前年比＋1％程度とまずまずの
成長率になる計算だ。 
年度後半の個人消費では、インフレ再加速による実質賃金の伸び悩みが消
費拡大の抑制要因になるだろう。 原油 ・ ナフサ価格高騰の影響が徐々に波及
し、 コア消費者物価指数 （生鮮食品を除く総合） は年度末にかけて前年比＋
3％程度まで上昇すると予想される（ただし、2026年末から政府の電気・ガ
ス料金支援策が実施されれば、0.4～0.5％Pt程度の押し下げ要因になる可能
性がある）。加えて、2027年4月の消費税減税開始前には、保存食品などを
中心とした買い控えの動きが生じるとみられ、 一時的に消費が落ち込む可能
性がある。 
他方で、年度後半の設備投資は堅調に拡大するだろう。日銀短観（9月調
査） では、2026年度の設備投資計画 （ソフトウェア・研究開発を含む設備投
資額 （除く土地投資額）） が前年比＋9.3％と高い伸びを維持した （6月 調 査：
同＋8.8％）。生産・営業用設備投資判断DIが2025年度後半以降に低下し、
設備の不足感が強まっていることをあわせて考えれば、 設備投資は今後拡大
傾向で推移していく見込みだ。 
また、政府の投資支援策である「大胆な投資促進税制」 （特定生産性向上
設備等投資促進税制）が2026年7月末から開始されたことも、今後の設備投
資にとってプラス要因になろう。同税制における減税規模の政府見込みを参
考に試算すると、2026～2028年度に設備投資の前年比変化率を約0.1％Ptず
つ押し上げる効果があると考えられる。 
ただし、 日銀が来年にかけて政策金利を一段と引き上げ、 企業の新規借入
金利がさらに上昇すると予想されること、 また、 投資財の価格が上昇傾向を
続けると考えられることを踏まえると、 中小企業を中心に設備投資を控える
企業が今後増加する可能性がある点には注意が必要だ。

### Page 5

5

日銀は10月会合で 政策金
利を据え置き 。次回利上
げは12月と予想 
10月29・30日に開催された金融政策決定会合において、 日本銀行は政策金
利を現行の1.25％で据え置いた。次の利上げ（1.25％→1.50％） は、次回12
月17・18日の会合と予想する。 前回利上げを行った9月会合に続いて 「3カ月
に1回」ペースでの利上げになり、 日銀の利上げペースが6月以前の 「半年強
に1回」から加速していることを印象付けるものになろう。 
企業の価格転嫁行動は一段と積極化しており、植田総裁が9月会合後の記
者会見で 「政策の局面が変化した」と説明したように、日本銀行は「物価引
き上げ」 から 「物価上振れ抑制」 へと金融政策運営の舵を切っている。12月
の追加利上げ後も、 金利上昇の影響を確認しながら、 日銀は最終的に2027年
半ばにかけて政策金利を2.0％まで引き上げていくだろう。 
なお、経済見通しの詳細については、みずほ総合研究所調査部「2026年・
2027年 みずほ世界経済見通し ─ 転換点は近いのか、投資主導経済の行方
を探る ─」（10月2日）をご覧いただきたい。 
（https://www.mizuhobank.co.jp/corporate/mhri/research/report/pdf/outlook261002.pdf）

### Page 3

3
Cathay’s View市場策略思維
市場焦點
1. 市場焦點:
• 重要企業公布財報, 牽動AI類股走勢: Anthropic發布IPO公開說明書, CSP與記憶體大廠陸續公布財報,
個股走勢分化。
• 央行按下暫停鍵, 但不代表升息週期結束: 美、日、歐央行均選擇按兵不動, 但12月升息機率仍偏高。
• 殖利率快速回落, 創2023以來單日最大跌幅: AI融資疑慮再次發酵, 實質利率回落使債券久違重拾避險
資產角色。
2. 後續看點:
• 11/2美國製造業PMI、11/3-11/4美國期中選舉、11/4美國服務業PMI、11/6美國非農與失業率
策略思維
1. 股市−選舉前波動易放大, 但長多趨勢尚未結束: 就歷史數據而言, 美國期中選舉前股市表現往往較差, 但AI
需求仍未見終點, 強勁基本面支撐下股市仍有高點可期。
2. 債市−殖利率飆升暫緩, 但高盤格局不易改變: 近期債券暫時重拾避險資產角色使殖利率明顯回落, 但AI相關
企業發債量攀升的趨勢仍在, 且通膨快速回落不易, 預期殖利率仍偏向高檔震盪。

3. 匯市−美元指數漲勢暫緩, 但強美元仍將維持一段時間: 雖近期美元指數漲勢趨緩, 但Warsh決心抗通膨並重
建聯準會獨立性, 加上歐洲動盪未平, 美元短期內轉弱難度偏高。

Cathay’s View
Anthropic營收成長驚人, 預估Q3營收超過上半年總和 Anthropic未來算力採購計畫
資料來源: Anthropic預估, 預估期間為2026Q3, 截至2026/10/26。 資料來源: Anthropic, 截至2026/10/26。
• Anthropic IPO公開說明書指出, 今年Q2營收116億美元, 年增近14倍, 季增144%, 並預估Q3營收168億美元, 年增
11倍, 季增45%, 超過今年上半年營收總和, 營收成長驚人。此外, Q3營收是同期算力支出的兩倍, 預估Q4營收是
算力支出的1.7倍, 展現公司強勁的AI營收變現能力。
• Anthropic亦定調公司核心策略, 未來將擴大投入, 透過提升模型、降低token成本, 帶動客戶使用量增加, 進而帶動
營收成長, 並再度擴大投資。公司預估至2036年, 將投入約8000億美元採購算力, 為近一年投資的50倍, 反映AI需
求展望強勁。

主要雲端服務商財報狀況
註: 基於公平比較, EPS指GAAP EPS數值。
資料來源: Bloomberg, 國泰世華銀行整理, 截至2026Q3財報。
• 隨著大型 CSP 業者陸續公布財報 , 除了企業獲利表現 , 自由現金流是否穩定或改善 , 也成為市場關注的焦點 。
SpaceX由虧轉盈, 自由現金流也大幅改善, 帶動股價於盤後大漲7.8%。微軟的自由現金流雖小幅下滑, 但仍穩定
維持在百億美元水準, 盤後股價同樣上漲。反觀Meta、谷歌與亞馬遜, 則因自由現金流惡化, 導致盤後股價重挫。
• 不過, 無論各家業者的股價表現如何分歧, 其共同點都是肯定AI需求依然強勁, 且已實質反映在營收成長上, 同時持
續擴大資本支出。其中, SpaceX雲端收入成長8倍, 谷歌雲端收入成長102%, 微軟Azure成長48%,亞馬遜AWS年增
39%, 均展現強勁的成長動能。

台股指數創新高, 但獲利成長更快, 估值反而下降 台積電資本支出快速上升
資料來源: Bloomberg預估, 截至2026/10/30。 資料來源: 台積電, JP預估, 截至2026/10/30。
• 台股指數雖拉回, 但企業獲利持續走高, 使台股本益比進一步降至近期低檔, 顯示AI投資造成資金吃緊、殖利率走
揚的現象, 對評價壓力加大。這也使得高本益比的族群拉回時壓力更大, 企業獲利成長性較佳的類股具下檔支撐。
• 台積電對於AI長期成長看法未變, 在持續擴大資本支出時也深入評估客戶端風險 , 由於未來3年台積電資本投資仍
將遠高於過去3年, 預料明年缺貨現象仍將普遍出現, 考量AI需求與企業獲利趨勢延續, 台股拉回仍可留意布局機會。

SK hynix獲利持續創新高, 營利率維持高檔 SK hynix財報會議重點
韓股｜匯率逆風下財報仍超預期, SK hynix重申樂觀展望
• SK hynix公布26Q3財報, 營業利潤83.3兆韓元、營業利潤率79.6%均創歷史新高, 均優於市場預期。儘管淨利因匯
兌與業外投資損失而不及預期, 但核心業務表現強勁, 股價大漲10.9%。
• 管理層正面釐清多項原先市場擔憂的議題, 並重申AI需求仍然極為強勁, 明後兩年供不應求的狀況將持續。雖從獲
利年增率的角度而言, 2027年很難優於2026年, 但回顧過去記憶體類股的歷史週期, 只要獲利能夠持續增長而不衰
退, 股價仍然存在上行空間。

經濟

升息

10年債利率跌回5%下方, 惟期限溢價仍高 TIPS回落, Breakeven相對平穩
資料來源: Bloomberg, ACM為紐約聯準會之期限溢價預估模型, 截至2026/10/27。
註:名目利率 ≈ 實質利率(TIPS) + 通膨預期(Breakeven Rate)。
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/10/27。
• 市場對AI投資回收進度與獲利轉換能力產生疑慮, 帶動全球科技股修正; 資金流向避險資產, 帶動美國10年期公債
殖利率於10/22單日下跌16.6bps, 目前於5%附近震盪。
• 從利率結構觀察, 10年Breakeven Rate大致維持在2.3%: 呼應主席Warsh所言, 油價拉高民眾「短期通膨預期」,
但中長期通膨指標依然錨定。相較之下, 10年TIPS持續攀升為Fed升息帶動實質利率走升、信用情勢收緊。
• 此外, 美國財政紀律、美國公債供給增加、AI Capex帶動企業發債需求, 仍將支撐期限溢價處於高檔; 雖短線長端

利率受買盤影響回落, 但結構性因素驅使長端利率中樞位居高位的情勢未改。

會計項目導致帳面巨額虧損, 但實際已出現淨獲利 Q2經調整營利率翻正, 反映獲利能力強勁
註: 公司估值上升, 使融資工具價值增加, 因此需認列帳面損失, 但不造成實質現金流出。
資料來源: Anthropic, 截至2026/6/30。
註: Non-GAAP營業利益率 = (GAAP營業利益-金融負債公允價值損益調整)/營收。
資料來源: Anthropic, 截至2026/6/30。
• Anthropic Q2 GAAP淨利為-663億美元, 但主因是隨估值增加, 公司融資工具帳面價值同步增加, 形成帳面損失, 但
不造成實際支出。排除會計評價損益調整後, 2026上半年non-GAAP淨利達27億美元, 獲利已轉正。
• 此外, Q2 non-GAAP營利率達19%, 預估Q3亦能維持正向, 反映公司獲利能力已有一定基礎, 未來可望持續加速。
未來投資人可關注Anthropic營收增速是否保持高速成長, 以及non-GAAP營利率與淨利是否持續改善, 作為公司

AI投資變現能力與算力需求前景觀察指標。

求轉化為實質營收。此外, 相較於其他CSP業者, 其自由現金流依舊穩定, 面對當前市場放大檢視財務面時, 預期微

• 谷歌第三季營收1275.8億美元, 較去年同期成長25%, 不如預期, 而業外投資虧損, 導致EPS僅2.12美元, 亦不如預
期。此外, 自由現金流缺口擴大90.7億美元, 亦遠超過市場預期的35億美元, 導致盤後股價重挫近10%。

務面仍承受一定的壓力 , 短期股價波動較大 。不過, 投資效益也逐步顯現 , 雲端收入年增率攀揚至 102%。此外,
Google具備自研TPU、Gemini模型等完整AI生態系, 有利於降低對外部晶片的依賴, 長線展望仍佳。

• 日本央行貨幣政策正常化速度仍然偏慢, 且明年將有兩位鷹派委員卸任, 預期後續升息幅度與速度均受到制約。美

與壽險具備資金回流本國資產誘因, 使日圓貶幅亦有支撐。多空因素疊加下, 預期日圓至年底仍應維持區間走勢。

通膨上行風險仍存 升息預期較9月會議有所降溫, 市場預估未來2~3碼空間
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/9。
註: 比較與9月會議之升息預期差異。
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/10/29。
• 歐洲央行(ECB)如預期維持三大利率不變 (DF/MRO/MLF分別維持在2.50%/2.65%/2.90%)。隨能源價格上漲推升
通膨上行風險與經濟下行壓力 ; 目前政策關鍵在於管控通膨風險 , 同時評估能源衝擊對實體經濟的影響性 。此外,
總裁Lagarde宣布將於明年2月提前卸任, 增添繼任角力與政策不確定性。
• 儘管面臨領導層異動, 仍預計ECB維持偏鷹政策立場: 10月聲明中強調基本通膨(Underlying inflation)大致維持穩定,
且薪資成長未顯著走升; 但若能源衝擊規模持續擴大, 仍需觀望是否產生二輪通膨效應。
• 隨美伊局勢持續膠著, 全球原油運輸與供應鏈不確定性高企。在通膨風險未完全消退下, 預計ECB將維持數據導向

與漸進升息的彈性空間; 目前期貨市場仍定價未來一年具2~3碼的升息空間, 相較9月會議略有降溫。

國家隊進出狀況 A股指數與評價走勢
中國｜外需撐起GDP, 政治邏輯重塑定價, 大型政策刺激暫且無望, 指數需時間築底
• Q3 GDP年增4.5%, 較上季4.3%回升, 前三季累計達4.6%, 落全年4.5~5%目標區間, 「穩中有升」反而讓政策刺激
期待落空。GDP結構並不均衡, 出口與高端製造良好; 但民間消費僅溫和擴張、投資持續下滑, 房地產依舊拖累。
• 另方面, 人民日報先以「全面從嚴治黨」為五中全會定調。將長征、遵義會議、毛澤東核心確立, 串聯至「兩個確
立」與「十五五」規劃。黨敘事把組織置於發展之上, 呼應「管黨治黨越有效, 經濟發展的保障越有力」的主張。
• 考量數據與治理取向, 1)短線行情偏淡、指數需時間築底, 因為大規模刺激期待落空, 加上國際氣氛讓AI硬體股跟

跌, 資金只能轉向金融股暫待; 2)中長線在黨建先於發展的邏輯下, 資金還是只能流向國企色彩高、與政策對齊如
半導體、能源安全、軍工等類股。

魯拉的政策方向對於財政負擔明顯較重 市場提前定價魯拉下台, 選情逆轉後股匯雙殺
巴西｜現任總統魯拉意外逆轉勝, 財政可持續性疑慮使巴西面臨股匯雙殺
• 巴西現任總統魯拉於第二輪投票中逆轉勝, 出乎市場預期。由於魯拉的政策將使巴西面臨較大的財政風險, 且原先
市場早已定價小波索納洛(其父2022敗選後被控政變罪, 遭判處監禁27年)將勝出, 故結果出爐後, 巴西的股匯市大
幅下跌。
• 根據最新消息, 小波索納洛拒絕承認選舉結果, 同時無視限制出境令前往美國尋求庇護。考量原先魯拉與川普關係
不睦, 不排除近期美國再對巴西祭出制裁措施, 美巴雙邊風險上升或將使近期巴西金融市場持續動盪。

2026年 10月 30日 全7頁

2026年 7-9月期 GDP（1次速報）予測 
～前期比年率＋1.5％を予想～ 
個人消費は小幅減だった一方、設備投資と輸出が実質GDPを押し上げ

経済調査部 エコノミスト 畑中 宏仁 
  チーフエコノミスト 神田 慶司

[要約] 
◼ 11月 16日公表予定の 2026年 7-9月期の GDP1次速報では、実質 GDPが前期比年率＋
1.5％（前期比＋0.4％）と、4四半期連続のプラス成長を予想する。個人消費は小幅
に減少したが、設備投資と輸出の増加が実質GDPを押し上げたようだ。 
◼ 個人消費はサービスが増加したものの、財が減少した影響の方が大きかったと予想す
る。設備投資は 4-6 月期に特殊要因で減少していた反動もあって増加したとみられる。
公共投資は減少、政府消費は増加したと見込んでいる。 
◼ 輸出と輸入はサービスが減少した一方、財が増加したと予想する。代替調達の進展に
よる鉱物性燃料輸入増加は実質 GDPを押し下げたが、石油国家備蓄の大規模放出が行
われなかったことで公的在庫変動が実質 GDP成長率を押し上げた分が相殺したようだ。

図表 1：2026年 7-9月期 GDP予測表

1．2026年 7-9月期の実質 GDP成長率は前期比年率＋1.5％を予想 
11月 16日公表予定の 2026年 7-9月期の GDP1次速報では、実質 GDPが前期比年率＋1.5％
（前期比＋0.4％）と、4四半期連続のプラス成長を予想する（10月30日正午までに公表された
基礎統計に基づく）。個人消費は小幅に減少したが、設備投資と輸出が実質GDPを押し上げたよ
うだ。 
個人消費はサービスが増加したものの、財が減少した影響の方が大きかったと予想する。設
備投資は4-6月期に特殊要因で減少していた反動もあって増加したとみられる。 
輸出と輸入はサービスが減少したものの、財が増加したと予想する。中東産原油の代替調達
の進展による鉱物性燃料輸入の増加は実質 GDPを押し下げたが、石油国家備蓄の大規模放出が
行われなかったことで公的在庫変動が実質GDP成長率を押し上げた分で相殺されたようだ。

図表 2：実質 GDP成長率と需要項目別寄与度の推移

民需：個人消費、住宅投資は減少の一方、設備投資は大幅な増加を予想 
個人消費は前期比▲0.1％と予想する。4-6月期のインバウンド（個人消費の控除項目）の実
績が上方改定されるであろうことを踏まえると、2 四半期連続のマイナスとなると見込んでい
る。サービスは増加したものの、耐久財と半耐久財、非耐久財がそろって減少したとみている。
耐久財では、自動車税の環境性能割廃止やエアコンの省エネ基準の引き上げの影響もあって 4-
6 月期に増加していた自動車や家電への支出が減少したようだ。半耐久財・非耐久財では衣服
やたばこ、ガソリンなどが増加したものの、食料品などが減少したとみられる。サービスは外
食や鉄道旅客、娯楽などが増加したようだ。 
住宅投資は前期比▲1.8％と予想する。国土交通省によると、7月の建設工事費デフレーター
（住宅総合、大和総研による季節調整値）が前月比＋6.8％と高い伸びを示す中にあっても、7、
8 月平均の新設住宅着工戸数（国土交通省による季節調整値）は、分譲住宅の増加に支えられ
-1.0

### Page 3

1

3 / 7

4-6月平均比＋1.0％となった（巻末の関連指標②上段右）。しかし、進捗ベースで計上される
GDP統計上は、建設資材等の価格上昇の影響により実質では減少したとみられる。 
設備投資は前期比＋2.0％と予想する。建設投資は減少したものの、機械投資と知的財産生
産物投資（ソフトウェア、研究開発など）が増加したようだ。研究開発投資の増加には、4-6
月期に特殊要因で研究開発サービスの受取（設備投資の控除項目）が大幅に増加したことの反
動も含まれる。経済産業省によると、資本財総供給指数は、国産品と輸入品が共に増加し、7-
9 月期は前期比＋4.9％となった（巻末の関連指標②中段左）。一方、建設投資は、国土交通省
によると、民間非居住用建築・土木の出来高（名目額、大和総研による季節調整値）の7、8月
平均は 4-6月平均比＋1.2％となったが、7月の建設工事費デフレーター（非住宅総合、大和総
研による季節調整値）は前月比＋7.3％と大きく上昇しており、実質では減少したとみられる。 
民間在庫変動は前期比寄与度▲0.0％ptと予想する。仮置き値の原材料在庫は同＋0.1％pt、
仕掛品在庫は同▲0.1％pt となった。その他では製品在庫が同＋ 0.1％pt、流通在庫が同▲
0.1％ptだったとみられる。

公需：公共投資は減少、政府消費は増加と予想 
公共投資は前期比▲1.0％と予想する。国土交通省によると、7、8 月平均の公共工事出来高
（名目額、大和総研による季節調整値）は 4-6月平均比▲2.2％と減少傾向にある（巻末の関連
指標②中段右）。25年度補正予算に盛り込まれた公共事業の執行が一服したとみられる。加え
て、工事費の上昇も重しとなっている。 
政府消費は前期比＋0.2％と予想する。退職金支払いの増加や私立高校授業料の実質無償化、
公立小学校の給食費無償化もあって高い伸びとなった 4-6月期に続き、医療費の増加により 7-
9月期の政府消費も増加したとみられる。 
公的在庫変動は前期比寄与度＋0.3％ptと予想する。4-6月期と異なり、7-9月期には大規模
な石油の国家備蓄放出は行われず、公的在庫変動は実質GDP成長率を押し上げたとみられる。

外需：輸入増が輸出増を上回り、外需寄与度は▲0.2％ptを予想 
輸出は前期比＋0.5％と予想する。サービスは減少したものの、財は増加したと見込んでい
る。財では半導体等製造装置や化学製品、鉱物性燃料などが増加したようだ。サービスでは、
4-6月期に大幅に増加していた研究開発サービスの反動減に加え、産業財産権等使用料やイン
バウンドなども減少したとみている。 
輸入は前期比＋1.8％と予想する。輸出と同様、サービスは減少したものの、財は増加した
と見込んでいる。財では、代替調達の進展により鉱物性燃料が大きく増加したほか、電算機類
も増加したようだ。サービスではアウトバウンドは増加したものの、研究開発サービスや著作
権等使用料などが減少したとみられる。

### Page 4

1

4 / 7

輸入増が輸出増を上回り、純輸出（外需）の実質GDP成長率に対する寄与度は前期比▲0.2％
ptになったと予想する。

2．10-12月期の実質 GDPはおおむね横ばいを予想 
2026年 10-12月期の実質 GDP成長率は前期比でおおむね横ばいになると見込んでいる。個人
消費は小幅に増加、設備投資は増加すると予想する。輸出はAI関連需要を背景とした米国向け
輸出を中心に増加するものの、輸入の伸びが上回り、外需はマイナスになるとみている。 
個人消費は小幅に増加すると予想する。厚生労働省「毎月勤労統計調査」の現金給与総額は
前年同月比で 3％を超える伸びが続いていることに加え、10月以降は各地で最低賃金が引き上
げられることから、名目賃金の高い伸びは続くだろう。一方、9月の消費者物価指数（除く生
鮮食品）は前年同月比＋2.1％と、8月から 0.4％pt伸び幅が拡大した。政策要因による影響を
差し引いたとしても、食料品等を中心に物価上昇率は高まっており、実質賃金上昇率を押し下
げるだろう。エネルギー価格についても、電気・ガス料金補助は12月使用分（1月請求分）か
ら再開されるものの、現行の補助は 9月使用分（10月請求分）で一旦終了するため、10-12月
期の物価は押し上げられる。加えて、ガソリン補助金の基準価格が12月以降引き上げられるこ
とや、燃料費・原料費調整制度によって資源価格高騰の影響がラグを持って電気・ガス料金に
反映されることも物価の押し上げ要因となろう1。 
設備投資は増加すると予想する。日本銀行「全国企業短期経済観測調査」 （日銀短観）によ
ると、9月調査時点での 2026年度の設備投資計画（全規模全産業、除く土地、含むソフトウェ
ア・研究開発）は前年度比＋9.3％と、前年の 9月調査時点（同＋9.5％）と同程度の伸びが示
された。建設資材価格と労務費の上昇が建設投資を下押しするものの、省力化投資やAI関連投
資といった設備投資の増加基調は続くと見込む。 
住宅投資は建設資材と労務費上昇の影響を受けて減少すると見込んでいる。公共投資は工事
費の高騰を背景に横ばい圏で推移すると予想する。政府消費は高齢化などに伴う医療費・介護
費増により増加すると見込んでいる。 
輸出は増加すると予想する。米国の設備投資は中東情勢をめぐる不透明感や金利上昇の中に
あっても、AI関連需要を背景に増加が続くだろう。米国内での設備投資の増加は米国向け輸出
の追い風となり、財輸出は増加すると見込んでいる。一方、インバウンドは中国政府による日
本への渡航自粛要請が続く中、小幅な増加にとどまるだろう。 
輸入は増加すると予想する。経済産業省によると、9月の原油調達について、バブ・エル・
マンデブ海峡経由で調達予定であった原油の輸送ルートをスエズ運河経由に切り替えたことで

1 12月以降の電気・ガス料金補助とガソリン補助金に関する政府の対応については、首相官邸「中東情勢を踏
まえた『緊急的激変緩和措置』の今後の対応についての会見」（2026年10月 29日）を参照。

### Page 5

1

5 / 7

到着が遅れた分は備蓄原油の既放出分で対応したという2。10月は前年平月比 10割の調達が実
現できる見通しとのことであり、11月以降も同量を確保できるのであれば、財輸入は押し上げ
られるだろう。 
リスク要因は中東情勢の悪化とAI関連需要の下振れである。中東情勢が悪化し、原油価格が
さらに高騰すれば、物価高を通じて個人消費を一段と下押しするだろう。また、足元の米国経
済では設備投資が景気を下支えしており、とりわけデータセンターや半導体などのIT関連投資
がけん引役となっている。10月半ばにかけて、一部AI関連企業の財務上の脆弱性が意識され、
株価が下落する局面があったが、こうした懸念がAI関連企業全体に広がれば、外部資金への依
存度が高い企業を中心に設備投資が鈍化し得る3。その場合、米国向け輸出が下押しされる可能
性があり、注意が必要だ。

2 詳細は、第 5回中東情勢に伴う重要物資の安定的な供給確保のためのタスクフォース（2026年 10月2日）
における経済産業省提出資料を参照。 
3 詳細は、矢作大祐・藤原翼「米国経済見通し CoreWeave問題をどうみるか」（大和総研レポート、2026年
10月22日）を参照。

Source:                         Morgan Stanley Research estimates.
In Taiwan, we prefer TUC over EMC for greater pricing upside in
the next 12 months, given its higher exposure to midrange/low-
end CCL. Both stocks are attractively valued: EMC at 11x CY28e
P/E and TUC at 9x.
EMC (2383.TW)
• EMC has introduced a new expansion plan for its total CCL capacity to reach
11.95mn sheets per month (SPM) in 2029 and 15.25mn in 2030 (+14% and

+28% y/y). See Exhibit 3  for EMC's 2026-2030 capacity plans.
• EMC's substrate CCL capacity currently sits at 300K SPM. The company
expects to add another 900K SPM in 2H27, reaching 1.2M SPM by end-2027.
Our supply chain checks suggest that a US hyperscaler  will be the first key
customer of EMC's ABF substrate CCLs for its  eighth-generation ASIC (see
our  TPCA takeaways).
• Management noted a growing trend of customers requesting long-term
agreements that span at least three years, on average. These L TAs now

secure volume rather than fixing prices; they do not require prepayments
from customers. Despite the flexibility in pricing and payment, customers
will still face punishment if they fail to pull their promised volumes.
• We estimate that 4Q revenue will grow ~19% q/q, to NT$72.4bn (+190% y/y),
supported by pricing adjustments and continued mix improvement, given
limited capacity increase in 4Q. We see GM expanding 220bps q/q, to 39.9%,
for EPS of NT$53.33 (+32% q/q).

TUC (6274.TWO)
• Management expects price hikes to continue into 4Q, with new pricing for its
M7/8 CCL taking effect from November onwards. For M6 and below, pricing
will remain driven by raw material cost pass-through amid supply tightness.
We expect this trend to persist into 2027, with HVLP4 copper foil, E glass,
and LDK2 glass cloth to be the most undersupplied.
• We believe recent market concerns about TUC's "share loss" for an ASIC
customer are overdone. Given the ongoing supply tightness in the high-end

CCL market, it is normal for CSPs to adopt multi-sourcing strategies to
diversify supply risks. Meanwhile, we believe TUC is actively engaging and
sampling high-end M8+ grade materials with other major CSP customer(s)
for next-gen AI projects. TUC's share in a key satellite customer's next-gen
satellite products will potentially expand in 2027 as well, with materials
upgraded to M7/8.
• We expect 4Q revenue to grow 15% q/q, to NT$20.6bn (+126% y/y), driven

by pricing adjustments and capacity addition at TUC's site in Thailand site
(+100K SPM in 4Q, +200K SPM in 3Q). We see GM expanding 50bps q/q, to
37.4%, for EPS of NT$15.82 (+20% q/q).
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of
interest that could affect the objectivity of Morgan Stanley
Research. Investors should consider Morgan Stanley

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.

TUC
3Q GM was 36.9% (+720bps q/q), 520bps above our estimate and 440bps above the
Street, primarily driven by improved  profitability of the plant in Thailand as well as CCL
price hikes, particularly for the lower grade, non-low loss CCL. We believe 3Q GM beat
even compared to buy-side consensus of 34-35%. As a result, 3Q OP came in at NT$5.6bn
(+66% q/q, +340% y/y), 13% above our estimate and 20% above the Street, for OpM of
31.0% (+750bps q/q). NP was NT$3,857mn (+65% q/q), only 5% above our estimate and

9% above the Street, owing to higher-than-expected tax rate, for EPS of NT$13.21.
TUC's plant in Thailand achieved positive GM in 3Q, driven by scale benefits; the plant's 3Q
profits have offset its losses in 1H26. That said, heavy flooding in Thailand since late
September has delayed a portion of shipments from this plant into October and
November.

19%,  Others 15%.

Price Target Discussion and Valuation Methodology
Our price target for EMC increases ~5%, to NT$8,800, driven by increases in our

2026-28 earnings estimates: Our new price target implies 20x CY28e P/E (unchanged).
Our price target is also our base case scenario value. It is derived from our residual income
(RI) model (similar to the rest of our tech hardware coverage).
To calculate the value of the stock, we use the company's beginning equity plus the
present value of all expected residual income of earnings in excess of cost of capital. The
RI is positive when ROAE is above the cost of capital and negative when it is below the

cost of capital. Our key assumptions (all unchanged) include a cost of equity of 9.3% (beta
of 1.0, equity risk premium of 8.7%, and risk-free rate of 1.0%), a medium-term growth rate
of 12%, and a terminal growth rate of 3%.
The 20x CY28 P/E implied by our price target is slightly above the past three years'
average +1SD of 18x, but much lower than the 32x recent P/E peak. We deem this
valuation fair because EMC is benefiting from an earnings growth cycle fueled by stronger

AI server CCL demand, EMC's own mix improvement, and continued pricing adjustments,
which should support margin expansion.
Our bull and bear case values also increase ~5%, to NT$13,650 and NT$4,200,
respectively.

TUC: Earnings Estimate Changes
We raise our  gross margin and operating margin assumptions for CY26-28 to factor in the
margin beat in 3Q26. However, we also revise our tax rate assumptions from 4Q26
onwards to reflect the higher-than-expected tax rate in 3Q26 (31.1% vs. our 25.0%
estimate). Our  EPS estimates consequently change +3% for CY26, -5% for CY27, and -6%
for CY28.

Price target discussion and valuation methodology
Our price target for TUC decreases  ~6%, to NT$2,550, primarily driven by the changes
in our earnings estimates for CY27-28: This implies 28x CY27e or 17x CY28e P/E. Our
price target is also our base case scenario value. It is derived from our residual income (RI)
model (similar to the rest of our tech hardware coverage).
To calculate the value of the stock, we use the company's beginning equity plus the

present value of all expected residual income of earnings in excess of cost of capital. The
RI is positive when ROAE is above the cost of capital and negative when it is below the
cost of capital. Our key assumptions (all unchanged) include a cost of equity of 9.3% (beta
of 1.0, equity risk premium of 8.7%, and risk-free rate of 1.0%), a medium-term growth rate
of 11%, and a terminal growth rate of 3%.
The 17x CY28e P/E implied by our price target is roughly in line with the past three years'

average +1SD of 17.8x. We deem this valuation fair because TUC is benefiting from an
earnings growth cycle fueled by stronger AI server/networking CCL demand, TUC's own
mix improvement, and continued pricing adjustments, which should support margin
expansion.
Our bull and bear case scenario values also decline ~6%, to NT$3,825 and NT$635,
respectively.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Key Data  _____________________________________

GS Forecast  __________________________________

GS Factor Profile  ______________________________

Win Semi’s 3Q26 core business (operating income) was 49/41% 
higher than GSe/BBG consensus, mainly driven by a more favorable 
product mix with higher contribution from the high margin 
infrastructure business (accounted for 39% of total revenue in 3Q26 
vs. 34% in 2Q26), resulting in a 9.2ppt QoQ increase in 3Q26 GPM, 
and came in 6.9/6.1ppt above GSe/BBG consensus. Win Semi’s 
OPEX increased to NT$775mn (vs. NT$743mn in 2Q26). NI was 
67/110% higher than GSe/BBG consensus due to the high 
non-operating income of NT$698mn (account for 33% of total 
pretax income). 
For 4Q26, the company expects revenue to increase by low single 
digit QoQ, and expects cellular to see QoQ decline due to weak 
season while the remaining businesses (including 
infrastructure/Wi-Fi/optical) will see flattish to small QoQ increase. 
For GM, the company guides the 4Q26 level to maintain at 
high-thirties %, which is much better than both our prior 
expectation and BBG consensus at 31.8/31.4%. Management 
indicated 3Q26 UTR to be higher at 75% (was 65% in 2Q26), and 
expect to maintain at similar level in 4Q26.  
The company continues to highlight the progress with its AI-related 
business, with its new PD (photodiodes) products started to ramp 
up from 2H26, with more contribution expected in 2027 along with 
more PD capacity coming online. For the new LD (laser diodes) 
products, the company continues to work with multiple clients on 
multiple new solutions (including CW laser, EML solutions and 
VCSEL), but expects to see more contribution in 2028 due to the 
lengthy reliability tests (takes 6M+ per our estimates). The company 
reiterated that CAPEX this year is project driven, which will mainly 
focus on expanding InP and GaN capacity for the datacom optical 
and infrastructure business.

### Page 3

Upward revision to PA foundry TAM reflecting foundry continue to gain
share despite weak smartphone shipment
We have revised up our 2026/27/28E P A foundry TAM by 5/4/2% to
US$1.3bn/1.5bn/1.6bn despite smartphone shipment remaining weak (GSe now at
-10%/+2%/+2% in 2026/27/28, was -10%/+3%/+1% previously, see here) as the
smartphone market remains exposed to the rising memory cost environment (see here).
The upward revision is based on our expectation that PA foundry house will continue to

gain share in the smartphone PA segment as newer smartphone projects from design
houses are being allocated to foundries due to the higher production costs and
increasing design complexity of the PA. This will benefit Win Semi in the mid to long term
(Win Semi’s revenue from cellular business already saw 28% YoY YTD). We now expect
the market TAM to deliver an 8% CAGR over 2026-30E, and we expect foundries’ cellular
PA shipments to increase by 11% in 2026 (Exhibit 2), outperforming our 2026

smartphone shipment estimates of -10% YoY due to the higher than expected PA
foundry penetration (Exhibit 5).
Win Semi delivered 3Q26 revenue that is better than the company’s guidance (actual
QoQ at 31% vs. guidance of low-teens QoQ), with the cellular business in peak season.
Management expects this momentum to slow down in 4Q26 and guided revenue to see
QoQ decline in the cellular business. We now expect Win Semi’s PA foundry revenue to
grow 31% YoY in 2026 (was 28% previously), outpacing the overall PA foundry TAM

growth of 22% YoY, as the company continues to focus on mid-to-high-end and
premium smartphone models (iOS camp accounts for 30-40% of the company’s cellular
business per our estimate), which are relatively more resilient to rising memory costs.
Moreover, we expect Win Semi’s PA foundry market share to decline from 74% in 2020
to 57% in 2026, and further lower to ~50% by 2030, but its revenue will continue to
expand further due to the growing foundry TAM.

We continue to expect Taiwan PA foundry players to see modest recovery in 2026
CAPEX. However, we do not view this as an indication of a recovery in demand for the
overall PA foundry industry. Win Semi maintained its 2026 CAPEX guidance of
NT$2-3bn, with the majority of spending allocated to the new InP and GaN capacity
rather than GaAs capacity expansion. We continue to believe GaAs capacity remains
sufficient as we estimate Win Semi’s quarterly utilization rate could reach at most 85%

throughout 2026-28E (Exhibit 1), suggesting no meaningful need for additional GaAs
capacity expansion.

### Page 5

Investment view 
The company has started to see increasing contribution from the new PD product in 
3Q26 but the contribution remains very limited (overall optical business only saw high 
single digit QoQ in 3Q26), while the company expects PD product revenue to further 
ramp up in 2027 along with more PD capacity coming online. This is tracking more slowly 
than our expectation, and we now expect the datacom optical business to account for 
only a mid-single-digit of 2026E revenue (was expecting high-single-digit previously). 
Meanwhile, progress in new high end LD products (including CW lasers, EML solutions 
and VCSEL) remains broadly the same since last earning call, with the company is still at 
verification stage and expect more meaningful contribution in 2028E. As such, we 
believe Win Semi’s revenue remains highly exposed to the potential weakness in 
smartphone demand, as we estimate smartphone related business (including cellular, 
WiFi, and optical) will account for 50%+ of total revenue in 2026E. The contribution

### Page 6

from datacom optical through 2026-27E could not reach scale, and it remains highly
uncertain whether optical companies will outsource LD products to Win Semi in 2028E.
As a result, we believe Win Semi’s stock price remains too elevated (trading at over 50x
2027E P/E based on GSe/BBG consensus) with limited contribution from the datacom
optical business.
We maintain our conservative view on Win Semi’s stock performance, given (1) the
contribution from datacom optical products (e.g., PDs and LDs) remains limited in

near-term, with the higher margin LD products potentially seeing more contribution in
2028E, (2) the company’s exposure in cellular business remains high (30%+), and the
smartphone market continue to be impacted by high memory cost; and (3) given the
slow technology upgrade speed in the GaAs foundry market, we believe competitors
such as AWSC could catch up with the technology and is better positioned to gain share
given their lower cost structures and more competitive pricing.

Maintain Sell, with a new 12-m TP of NT$162 (from NT$142).

### Page 7

Earnings revisions  
We raise our 2026E revenue by 2% after factor in 3Q26 results and raise our 2027/28E 
revenue by 5/6% to factor in higher infrastructure and optical business contribution. We 
revise up our 2026/27/28E GM estimates by 3.8/2.1/1.6ppt as we expect more margin 
expansion driven by higher UTR and the higher contribution from high margin 
infrastructure business. Overall, we revised up our 2026/27/28E EPS estimates by 
47/6/7%. 
Also, we introduce our 2029E earnings estimates with this report.

Valuation 
We maintain our Sell rating, and revise up our TP from NT$142 to NT$162, based on the 
same target PE of 24x (1x STDV lower than the industry upcycle average valuation), 
factoring in our earnings revisions and, consistent with our group coverage practices, roll 
over our valuation period from 4Q26-3Q27 to 2027E.

Investment Thesis and TP & risks

Win Semi is a key GaAs-based semi foundry player with c.49% global market share as of 
2025. We believe the company’s market share could decline given its much 
higher-than-peer product pricing in the demand downtrend. We also expect the

### Page 8

company to be impacted by the slow PA foundry market growth outlook given weak
end-market demand and high inventory levels. This is despite some longer-term demand
drivers (China PA in-sourcing trend, WiFi 6E/7 upgrading trend, new smartphone/AR VR
optical products, automotive LiDAR opportunity, satellite communication market
opportunity, stronger 5G BTS deployment in EU/US market, datacom optical
opportunity etc.), which should increase its revenue/GM in the long term. However, we

believe Win Semi’s market share loss will continue to weigh on its long-term growth
opportunity. We are Sell-rated given the ongoing PA foundry industry downcycle, along
with our cautious view on the company’s market share outlook. Win Semi is now trading
above its average downcycle P/E multiple. Key upside risks are (1) easing competition in
the China PA foundry market, (2) faster-than-expected progress on 5G/WiFi 7 upgrade
trend, and (3) faster-than-expected growth from LEO/AI-related business.

Our 12-month target price of NT$162 is based on 24x 2027E EPS, which is 1x STDV
lower than the industry upcycle average valuation since 2016. Key upside risks are (1)
easing competition in the China PA foundry market, (2) faster-than-expected progress
on 5G/WiFi 7 upgrade trend, and (3) faster-than-expected growth from LEO/AI-related
business.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

Taiwan Semis: GUC (3443.TW): Mgmt. Call takeaways: Server CPU ramp 
to sustain momentum into 2027; opportunities across applications
31 October 2026 | 12:35AM HKT
We talked to GUC (3443.TW, not covered) management recently. The company’s Sep 
revenues were up 22% MoM (vs. +1% MoM in Aug), reflecting its revenues from CPU 
of a leading US CSP in strong ramp up with more foundry capacity rolling out, and 
would continue to support the growth in 4Q26 and 2027 per the company. The 
positive tone echoes our positive view on Global Servers TAM, which we expect 
leading US cloud service providers (CSPs) in aggregate will grow their capex budgets 
by 84%/ 52%/ 13% YoY in 2026-28E, with our estimates being raised by 4% / 17% / 
22% in 2026-28E to US$1,397bn by 2028E. Revenues in 3Q26 are driven by both 
NRE and turnkey solutions, with turnkey solutions revenues up 44% QoQ on the CPU 
ramp up, and despite turnkey solutions up to ~90% of 3Q26 revenues, the GM 
sustained at 20%+ in 3Q26, thanks to higher-margin NRE milestones. GUC adjusts 
pricing to reflect rising wafer and memory costs, and management sees memory 
impact is manageable on its customers’ projects. On competition, management 
highlighted its position as a core partner of TSMC, with in-house IP for 3D stacking as 
a key differentiator. GUC’s strong turnkey pull-in from its CSP customer’s server CPU 
and its focus on IP for TSMC 3D stacking point to continued demand for TSMC’s 
(2330.TW, Buy) advanced nodes and packaging. The positive tone on ASIC demand 
also echoes our positive view on rising ASIC penetration rate (~60% by 2028E, report 
link), supported by leading CSPs diversifying thier AI chips platforms and reducing 
token per second per costs, driving inferencing / training efficiency.

Company profile: GUC (3443.TW, not covered) is a ASIC design service company 
and a TSMC affiliate, providing NRE design services and turnkey production for

### Page 2

custom chips manufactured at TSMC. Its customers span cloud (CPUs, AI accelerators,
BMCs), automotive and consumer (image sensors, SSD controllers), with 68% of its
products under 7nm.
Key takeaways
1. Mass production as the key profit driver: Management noted that the key to
profitability lies in converting NRE projects into mass production. With the largest
customer’s CPU now in volume production, turnkey has become the main growth driver.
NRE carries higher GM than turnkey, but as it is recognized by milestone, quarterly NRE

margin varies with the mix of projects at different stages.
2. Expanding opportunities across cloud and automotive: GUC continues to open up
new opportunities across applications. In cloud, it works with all major US CSPs on
projects spanning CPUs and GPUs and also supplies other server projects such as BMCs,
while actively pursuing further AI accelerator opportunities. In automotive, it is engaged
with both US and Chinese customers at the NRE stage, two of which already plan for

mass production.

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가현저한 저평가 - 기업.pdf

### Page 1

00

삼성전자 (005930/KS) 
현저한 저평가 
3Q26 영업이익 106 조원 예상 
삼성전자의 3Q26 영업이익을 106조원 (+18% QoQ)으로 예상한다. 이는 기존 당사 
전망치 111 조원 대비 4.5% 하향된 것이다. 원/달러 환율 가정을 기존 1,450원에서 
1,400원으로 3.4% 하향, MX/NW 사업부의 적자 폭을 확대 반영 (-0.5조원 -> -
1.4조원)했기 때문이다. 주가의 핵심인 메모리 판가는 DRAM +17%, NAND +15%
로 오히려 예상을 소폭 상회할 것으로 예상된다. 출하는 수요 강세에도 불구하고 재고 
부족, HBM 확대에 따른 제한적 공급 여력으로 DRAM +4%, NAND +7%의 기존 예
상을 유지한다. HBM은 분기 최대 출하를 경신하기 시작할 것이다. 또한 AI 강세로 파
운드리 수주 및 가동률의 동반 상승이 지속 중이다. 성과 충당금 반영 등에 따라 단기 
흑자전환 가시성은 제한적이나, DS 사업부 전체적으로 강화되고 있는 국면이다.

2027 년 568 조원 (+55% YoY) 전망 유지. 환율 하락 반영 
SK증권은 삼성전자의 2027년 영업이익 전망치를 568조원으로 유지한다. 원/달러 환
율 가정을 기존 1,440원에서 1,340원으로 7% 하향했음에도, 실적 전망을 유지하는 
이유는 AI 메모리 수요 강세 속 공급 부족 심화, LTA에 따른 Dual market 효과에 의
한 Commodity 메모리 가격 강세, 2027년 HBM 판가 강세 (+103% YoY) 와 출하 
강세, 수익성 회복의 동반, 파운드리 적자 폭 축소의 본격화 전망 때문이다. 2027년 메
모리 출하는 DRAM +22%, NAND +16%, 판가는 DRAM +38%, NAND +28%
를 예상한다. 시장에서 우려하는 메모리 가격 하락, 감익 시나리오는 없을 것이다.

차별적 실적과 주주환원 본격화. 현저한 저평가 
SK증권은 메모리 업황과 삼성전자의 주가가 현저히 저평가 되어있다고 판단한다. 삼성
전자의 2027E P/E는 4.2X에 불과하며, 이익 창출력은 글로벌 최상위권이다. 주주환
원 정책 또한 기존의 FCF 50% 환원이 향후에도 유지될 것으로 가정한다면, 2027년 
193조원, 2028년 222조원으로 현재 시가총액 (우선주 포함) 대비 각각 11%, 13% 
수준에 육박한다. 성장을 위한 투자를 확대하고 있음에도, 대규모의 환원이 가능하다. 
LTA는 AI 시대 메모리 수요의 질과 실적 가시성을 제고시키며, 이는 주주환원의 지속
가능성을 의미한다. 메모리 가격의 높은 기저, LTA 물량의 계약 가격 적용을 감안하면, 
가격 상승률 자체는 안정화될 것이다. 다만 실적 안정성, 가시성, Duration을 확보하기 
시작했다는 점이 훨씬 중요한 가치이며, 아직 시장의 Playbook에 없을 뿐이다. 시대는 
변하고 있고, 시장도 변해야 한다. 투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 610,000원을 유지한다. 
2026-10-06

반도체. 한동희 / donghee.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-8826

본 보고서에 언급된 종목의 경우 당사 조사분석담당자는 본인의 담당종목을 보유하고 있지 않습니다.

주가 추이

3분기 영업 이 익 은 지상방산 매출 감소와 일 회성 비용 등의 영 향 으로 
기대치를 대폭 하회했습니다. 4분기 실적 회복 과 중 장기 실적 성장 대
비 저평가된 밸 류 에이 션 에 주목합 니 다. 
3Q26 Review: 영업이익 0.5조원으로 컨센서스 대폭 하회 
3분기 매출액은 6.1조원으로 전년대비 6.0% 감소했다. 항공우주 부문
과 연결자회사들 성장에도 불구하고 지상방산 매출이 전년대비 약 0.9
조원 감소한 영향이다. 지상방산 내수와 수출 매출은 각각 0.7조원, 0.6
조원으로 전년대비 28.6%, 50.4% 감소했다. 내수는 대전 사업장 생산 
중단, 수출은 4분기 납기 집중에 기인한다. 항공우주 매출은 군수사업 
물량 증가로 전년대비 9.3% 증가한 0.7조원을 기록했다. 연결 자회사
인 한화오션과 한화시스템 매출은 각각 3.4조원, 0.8조원으로 전년대비 
12.9%, 30.1% 증가했다. 영업이익은 0.5조원으로 전년대비 49.2% 감
소했으며 영업이익률은 7.4%로 6.3%P 하락했다. 한화오션과 한화시스
템의 이익 증가에도 지상방산 이익 감소와 필리조선소 적자 반영이 영
향을 미쳤다. 지상방산은 일회성 비용 약 460억원, 원화 강세, 매출 감
소에 따른 고정비 부담 증가가 복합적으로 작용했다. 세전이익은 한화
오션의 외화환산손실 영향으로 -0.2조원을 기록하며 적자전환했다.  
실적 회복과 저평가 매력에 주목할 시점 
4분기 영업이익은 1.7조원으로 추정하며 시장 기대치를 상회할 전망이
다. 지상방산 수출 인도 물량이 집중되는 영향이다. 2027년 예상 실적 
기준 P/E는 12.5배로 경쟁사 Rheinmetall(22.5배) 대비 낮다. 약 35조
원의 방산 수주잔고를 기반으로 최소 2029년까지 실적 성장이 예상되
는 만큼 저평가 매력이 높다. 신규 수주 시점과 실적에 대한 우려가 상
존하지만 현재 밸류에이션에는 상당 부분 반영된 것으로 판단한다. 미
국 중간선거 등 국방예산 관련 정책 불확실성과 환율 부담이 완화되면 
투자자들의 관심은 4분기 실적과 2027년 신규 수주로 이동할 전망이
다. 연말 주요국의 국방예산 확정은 신규 수주 시점에 대한 불확실성을 
낮추는 계기가 될 수 있다.  
투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 1,300,000원 유지 
글로벌 재무장 수요 확대에 따른 동사의 중장기 성장 전망은 여전히 
유효하다. 투자의견 BUY와 목표주가 1,300,000원을 유지한다.

[ Compliance Notice ] (공표일: 2026 년 10 월 30 일) 
이 자료는 조사분석 담당자가 객관적 사실에 근거해 작성하였으며, 타인의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 
반영했습니다. 본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 
자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (채운샘) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
한화에어로스페이스는 당사와 ‘독점규제 및 공정거래에 관한 법률’ 에 따른 계열회사 관계에 있는 법인입니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

### Page 6

한화에어로스페이스 (012450) [한화리서치]

6 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

[ 당사 조사분석자료의 투자등급 부여 비중 ] (기준일: 2026 년 9 월 30 일)

### Page 1

SK 스퀘어 (402340/KS) 
하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후가 더 기대 
예정보다 빠른 SK 하이닉스 자사주 매입 
SK스퀘어의 자회사인 SK하이닉스는 지난 8월 20일부터 11월 19일까지 자사주 40
조원(2,407만주, 3.3%)을 매입 예정이다. 9월 29일까지 1,715만주, 예정 매입수량 
대비 71.3%를 매입 완료하였다. 매입 초기 매일 65만주 매입하다가 9월 10일부터는 
60만주를 매입하고 있는데, 이러한 추세가 유지된다면 10월 중순경 자사주 매입이 마
무리될 예정이다. 자사주 매입 후 소각 예정으로 소각이 완료되면 SK스퀘어의 SK하아
닉스에 대한 지분율은 20.0%에서 20.7%로 상승하게 된다.

자사주 매입 이후 추가적인 주주환원에 대한 기대 
SK하이닉스는 지난 8월 19일 정책기간(‘25년~’27년) 동안 발생하는 누적 FCF의 
50% 이상을 주주환원 하는 주주환원 정책을 발표한 바 있다. 26년 SK하이닉스의 예
상 FCF는 164조원(컨센서스 기준)으로 50%를 주주환원 할 경우 자사주 매입 금액
(40 조원)을 제외한 주주환원 재원은 42 조원이다. 잔여 주주환원 재원이 모두 배당으
로 지급된다면 SK 하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후 SK 스퀘어의 지분율은 20.7%이므로 
SK스퀘어의 배당수입도 8.7조원에 달한다. 지난 3월 SK스퀘어는 ‘26년~’28년 경
상배당수입의 30% 이상을 주주에게 환원하는 정책을 발표했다. 보수적으로 30%만 
가정하더라도 SK스퀘어의 배당재원은 2.6조원(현 시가총액 대비 1.8%)이다. SK스퀘
어는 연초 주주총회에서 배당가능이익 확보 차원에서 자본준비금 5 조 8,900억원을 
이익잉여금으로 전입한 바 있다. 이는 26년 재무제표가 확정되는 27년 3월 이후 배당
가능이익으로 편입되므로 SK스퀘어의 주주환원 확대가 기대된다.

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 185 만원(유지) 
SK스퀘어의 순자산 가치 중 98% 이상이 SK하이닉스 지분가치이다. SK하이닉스는 
자사주 매입 이후 17.7% 주가가 상승했으나, SK 스퀘어 주가는 11.4% 상승했다. SK
하이닉스 자사주 매입·소각은 SK 스퀘어의 보유 지분가치 상승뿐 아니라 지분율도 상
승하기 때문에 자사주가 소각될 경우 SK스퀘어의 주가가 탄력적으로 움직일 가능성이 
높다. SK스퀘어 현재 NAV 대비 할인율은 43.7%, 12MF PBR 1.4배로 7월 이후 주
가 조정으로 밸류에이션 매력도 높아졌다. 투자의견 매수와 목표주가 185만원을 유지
한다.

당사는 자료공표일 현재 해당기업과 관련하여 특별한 이해 관계가 없습니다.

Document 329: European bond spreads in a duel with the ‘Bad’

Source PDF: all report pdf/European bond spreads in a duel with the ‘Bad’.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-04
Pages: 9
Status: ok

### Page 1

Fiscal strains and election risks are putting eurozone bonds to
the test. France is the key fault line, while Bunds’ safe-haven
appeal masks mounting supply pressures. We see a turbulent
ﬁrst half of 2027, and any relief hinges on a credible path to
French ﬁscal consolidation
Benjamin Schroeder
Credible French ﬁscal consolidation plans could bring relief to eurozone
government bond markets
European government bond markets – in a
duel with the “Bad”

European government bond spreads have returned to the market’s
focus. Importantly, spread moves are not purely idiosyncratic
country stories. They also reﬂect broader risk sentiment, and higher
interest rate volatility generally pushes spreads wider. At the onset
of the Middle East turmoil, for instance, we saw sovereign spreads
become more closely correlated with energy prices and
expectations for European Central Bank rate hikes.

### Page 2

But high deﬁcits – the “Bad”– and existing ﬁscal imbalances have
made some countries more vulnerable than others, and these
factors are increasingly shaping the dynamics of sovereign spreads.
The “Bad” has re-entered the scene and is once again dominating
the narrative in parts of the European government bond market.
While France is already being singled out as a risk factor with the
potential to change the broader outlook for European sovereign
debt, Europe’s busy election calendar suggests that idiosyncratic

drivers could become more important overall. Spain goes to the
polls later this month, and Italy will have to hold legislative
elections before the end of next year. Finland will also hold
parliamentary elections, while Germany faces elections in ﬁve
federal states. And that is just to name the most prominent.
As markets navigate these political and ﬁscal risks, we expect
spreads to remain at wider levels through much of the ﬁrst half of
the year.

Markets have reordered the EMU countries
over the years, and have now singled out
France
Source: IMF, Reﬁnitiv, ING
France – Facing the very “Bad”, the one
that could change it all
Moody’s recently aﬃrmed France’s last remaining double-A rating
but maintained a negative outlook. The underlying assumption is
that a 2027 budget containing at least some ﬁscal consolidation

### Page 3

will eventually pass, even if this requires the use of a decree. That
would represent a step in the right direction and could oﬀer French
government bond spreads some welcome relief going into the new
year.
Beyond that, however, a “signiﬁcant and sustained ﬁscal eﬀort” will
still be required. The outlook is clouded by the 2027 presidential
election, with the two voting rounds on 18 April and 2 May. The far-
right’s Marine Le Pen, the current leading contender, could

subsequently call snap legislative elections in the summer in an
attempt to break the parliamentary deadlock.
The benign scenario would be one in which Le Pen wins and follows
through on her promises of ﬁscal consolidation. In that case,
spreads could return towards levels seen before September, and
France could conceivably trade tighter than Italy again. There is,
however, still a long way to go before such an outcome can be
taken for granted.
If Italy is the yardstick for a proper crisis,

there is still room for escalation
Source: Reﬁnitiv, ING
The consolidation plans presented so far by Le Pen appear
unrealistic, while the French public does not yet seem prepared to
support the scale of adjustment that would ultimately be required.
Against that backdrop, the market is likely to continue demanding
a sizeable political risk premium. In the absence of positive

### Page 4

catalysts, we see a 10-year OAT-Bund spread in the area of 150bp
as a ﬂoor during the period of electoral uncertainty.
The tail risk is that public discontent instead translates into stronger
support for the far left and Jean-Luc Mélenchon, whose proposals
have included highly controversial ideas such as cancelling French
debt held by the Bank of France. A greater probability being
attached to such an outcome could push the 10-year OAT-Bund
spread beyond 200bp.

More broadly, the absence of a credible multi-year consolidation
plan, combined with the possibility of another hung parliament,
would leave the door open to further sovereign rating downgrades,
and not just from Moody’s. In an unscheduled update, Fitch only
recently lowered the outlook of France’s A+ rating to negative.
Under such circumstances, spreads could remain noticeably above
100bp even once the immediate electoral uncertainty has passed.

That said, we believe current spread levels already extend beyond
what fundamentals alone would imply. The market is eﬀectively
pricing French debt as though it were already rated BBB. But
prolonged and exceptionally high country-speciﬁc uncertainty,
combined with elevated volatility in broader interest-rate markets,
keeps valuations unattractive for many investors. One big risk is
that structural foreign investors, which have historically been
attracted by France's large, liquid bond market, start abandoning

the credit. While data through August do not show any larger
shifts, Japanese investors, for instance, have already shown
themselves to be sensitive to French election risk in the past.
French has a large foreign investor base
which has looked ﬁckle around elections

### Page 5

Source: Bank of Spain, Bank of Japan, ING
The ECB can cap spreads, but does it
want to?
In theory, the European Central Bank has both the
instruments and the ﬁrepower to rein in sovereign spreads.
But as long as the turmoil remains largely contained to
France, we think the central bank will be inclined to let market
mechanisms play out, thereby maintaining pressure on
French politics to address the underlying ﬁscal issues. The
ECB has historically been careful to avoid creating the

impression of moral hazard.
If needed, the ﬁrst and least targeted response would be to
dial back hawkishness or even halt the tightening cycle. That
might appear an easy way to calm markets. But as long as
energy prices remain elevated and concerns about second-
round inﬂation eﬀects persist, there is a limit to how far the
ECB would be willing to go solely in response to wider
sovereign spreads.
A more targeted option would be to suspend quantitative

tightening by resuming reinvestments. The ECB could even
frame such a move as part of a broader adjustment of its
balance-sheet policy, particularly as excess liquidity in the
banking system approaches levels at which demand for
central bank reﬁnancing is expected to become more
important. In theory, liquidity operations should take over at
that stage. But as long as borrowing from the ECB continues
to carry some stigma for banks, ending QT and resuming

reinvestments could address two issues at once: easing

### Page 6

pressure on sovereign bond markets while also slowing the
decline in excess liquidity.
If turmoil were to spread further beyond France and threaten
the transmission of monetary policy, the ECB could
ultimately deploy its Transmission Protection Instrument. TPI
comes with eligibility considerations, including compliance
with the EU ﬁscal framework and debt sustainability, both of
which would clearly matter in the case of France, although
the ECB retains a degree of discretion in its assessment.

We think the bar for the ECB to actively intervene is high,
however. By comparison, when the ECB intervened directly in
Italian government bonds under the Securities Markets
Programme in 2011, 10-year BTP-Bund spreads had moved
beyond 500bp.
French bond spreads are already wider than
fundamentals alone would imply
Source: Reﬁnitiv, rating agencies, ING
Germany hides in a temporary safe haven
from the “Bad”
Germany sits at the opposite end of the spread spectrum from

France. Bunds have reasserted their traditional role as a safe haven
during the turmoil in French government bonds. During the
geopolitical tensions at the start of the Iran conﬂict, evidence of
such safe-haven demand had been ﬂeeting. This time, the richness

### Page 7

of Bunds looks more persistent because the turmoil goes to the
heart of some of the monetary union’s longstanding vulnerabilities.
Yet the structural forces that had previously driven the cheapening
of German government bonds have not disappeared.
Bond supply is likely to increase again as the government
continues to roll out its investment and defence spending agenda.
The German Finance Agency’s funding head has indicated that
gross bond funding could exceed €400bn next year, around €50bn

more than this year and making Germany the largest issuer of the
union. Higher redemptions mean that the increase in net issuance
is likely to be only around half as large. But the market must also
absorb the German government bonds running oﬀ the ECB’s
balance sheet. We estimate that this could add at least another
€80bn to the amount the private sector needs to absorb over the
coming year.
At the same time, political instability threatens the government’s

reform agenda precisely when Germany needs to reset its
economic model. An already fragile governing coalition will face
ﬁve state elections, four of them concentrated in April and May and
the other in September.
Once the immediate ﬂight-to-quality premium fades, we expect 10-
year Bunds to cheapen back to at least 20bp over OIS, from around
13bp currently. Domestic ﬁscal and political factors could push the
spread wider still by the end of the year.

10y Bund safe haven dynamics outweigh
structural issues for now

2027, likely a showdown in two parts
The ﬁrst half of next year will still be dominated by eurozone

political risk. France’s 2027 budget might oﬀer temporary but
welcome relief at the start of the year, when an important
funding window traditionally opens for many issuers beyond
the government bond market.
The second half of the year may oﬀer relief from the turmoil,
largely contingent on the outcome of the French elections
and whether a new president can oﬀer a credible path
towards ﬁscal consolidation. But at that point, Germany, as a
core issuer, becomes exposed again to its domestic structural

cheapening factors.

Style Exposure
We are incrementally positive on Jentech with multiple catalysts ahead. Near-term,

we believe the company’s 3Q26 earnings could be the first driver with a potential
GM beat, which should be reported in the second week of Nov. Longer-term, we
believe the drivers are from the incremental adoption of the removable lid in some
Nvidia Rubin GPUs in 2027 (in addition to Rubin Ultra), followed by the
meaningful Google TPU v9 spec upgrade potential in 2028 as another multi-fold
content increase story (based on JPMe), along with a structural margin expansion

trend given continued GPU and ASIC upgrades. Thus, we revise up our 2027E and
2028E EPS materially and are now 28% and 18% above the current BBGe
consensus.
• 3Q26 preview: expecting a stronger GM growth; 4Q26 outlook: slightly
better rev growth. We revise up our 3Q26 GM to 50.1% (vs current BBGe
consensus at 48.7%), which implies another quarter of strong GM expansion
(vs 2Q/1Q26 GM at 46.8%/41.8%), driven by better product mix to more AI
server business. Reported 3Q26 revenue of NT$9.5bn was largely in line with

the company’s guidance, and the 30% QoQ growth was mainly driven by the
decent ramp of Rubin GPU. As the Rubin 1-piece lid shipment should remain
at high volume into 4Q26, we believe the company’s revenue momentum will
also continue, with incremental growth from small volume of removable lid
shipment. Hence, we are now modeling Jentech’s 4Q26 revenue to grow 3%
QoQ, with GM further expanding thanks to ongoing mix enhancement.
• Further 2027 upside from incremental Rubin removable lid adoption;

2028 another earnings level-up year driven by meaningful upgrade in
TPU v9.We revise up our 2027E and 2028E revenue estimates materially by
22% and 14%, which are now 24% and 16% above consensus, respectively. For
the 2027 upward revision, it mainly reflects the incremental adoption of
removable lid (3-piece lid) in partial Rubin GPU from the next 1-2 quarter, in
addition to Rubin Ultra, as long as the company can ramp up its yield and
production of removable lid in the next few months with on-schedule capacity

expansion (new Taoyuan Aerotropolis plant) and equipment move-in (e.g. the
in-house screw locking equipment). More encouragingly, we believe 2028 will
see even greater earnings upside with Google TPU v9 likely upgrading its heat
spreader solution significantly to 2-piece or 3-piece lid design, in our view, as
the potential chip size (4-die) and TDP (potentially 2k+W or even higher)
should be a big level-up compared to v8, which makes the current stiffener-

only solution insufficient. As the content of 2-piece or 3-piece lid solution
could be 5-10x higher than the v8 stiffener, Google TPU v9 (with meaningful
volume) could drive the next wave of the earnings upgrade cycle for Jentech
from 2028 (following Nvidia Rubin/Rubin Ultra as the major driver in 2027).
• Implications. We lift our 2027E and 2028E EPS by 28% and 17% to NT$193
and NT$295, respectively, reflecting the incremental removable lid adoption

Summary Investment Thesis and Valuation
Investment Thesis
Jentech (OW) is the largest packaging-level cooling component
maker worldwide, with leading production capabilities across
heat spreader products (including stiffener and all kinds of lids).
We expect the company to show a ~90% EPS CAGR over 2024-
28, driven by solid heat spreader content growth in future-gen AI

chips and increasing TAM into new solutions (e.g., CPO switch
ASIC). In addition, the company is reinforcing its product
portfolio with new offerings in cold plate and CPU server
sockets for chip customers. We believe Jentech’s technology
leadership in the structural cooling upgrade trend and its new
TAM expansion will continue to support its superior earnings
power in the long term.
Valuation
Our Dec-27 PT of NT$8,800 is based on 30x 2028E P/E, which

is largely in line with Jentech’s three-year average P/E when the
AI server boom started.

Valuation
We derive our Dec-27 price target of NT$8,800 based on a 30x of 2028E P/E. This is
largely in line with Jentech’s historical average, as we expect the earnings upgrade cycle
to support this valuation.

Investment Thesis, Valuation and Risks
Jentech Precision Industrial Co. (Overweight; Price Target: NT
$8,800.00)
Investment Thesis
Jentech (OW) is the largest packaging-level cooling component maker worldwide, with
leading production capabilities across heat spreader products (including stiffener and all
kinds of lids). We expect the company to show a ~90% EPS CAGR over 2024-28, driven

by solid heat spreader content growth in future-gen AI chips and increasing TAM into new
solutions (e.g. CPO switch ASIC). In addition, the company is reinforcing its product
portfolio with new offerings in cold plate and CPU server sockets for chip customers. We
believe Jentech’s technology leadership in the structural cooling upgrade trend and its new
TAM expansion will continue to support its superior earnings power in the long term.
Valuation

Our Dec-27 PT of NT$8,800 is based on 30x 2028E P/E, which is largely in line with
Jentech’s three-year average P/E when the AI server boom started.
Risks to Rating and Price Target
Downside risks to our rating and price target include: 1) Potential delay in mainstream 3-
piece/removable lid design adoption, and 2) market share loss from competition.

MIDDLE EAST
BULLETIN
Persistent energy pressures have further to run
19 October 2026
Key points
• Shipping disruptions, depleted
inventories and elevated freight
costs are keeping physical crude
and fuel markets tight despite a

reported recovery in Gulf exports.
• Brent is forecast to average
US$101/bbl in Q4 before easing
to US$77/bbl in Q4 2027.
• Refined product markets are
expected to ease more slowly than
crude, with elevated gasoil cracks,
shipping and insurance costs slowing
declines in petrol and diesel prices.
• Under an escalation scenario,
renewed conflict and deeper supply
disruptions could push Brent towards
US$150/bbl in H1 2027, prolonging
elevated fuel and LNG prices.

• Under an early resolution scenario, a
durable US-Iran agreement accelerates
supply recovery, taking Brent towards
US$65/bbl by Q4 2027 and bringing
fuel and LNG prices down faster.
Q4 2026 forecasts:
Brent crude
US$101/bbl
Japanese LNG
US$24.5/MMBtu

### Page 2

are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
Low inventories and higher shipping costs will keep oil and
fuel prices elevated, with Brent futures forecast to average
US$101/bbl in Q4 before easing to US$77/bbl by Q4 2027
as supply recovers. Refined fuels and LNG prices should

ease more slowly. Risks are two-sided: escalation could
push Brent towards US$150/bbl, while an early US-Iran
agreement could see a faster normalisation in supply with
prices returning close to pre-conflict levels by end-2027.
This article was first published in the October Market Outlook .
Brent crude is back above US$100/bbl on fading confidence
in a lasting US-Iran agreement and intermittent attacks on
shipping. Buffers remain depleted, with the IEA estimating

global observed inventories were 507mb lower at the end of
August than at the start of the conflict.
This comes despite reported crude exports through the Strait of
Hormuz recovering to around 70% to 90% of pre-conflict levels
in late September. Saudi and UAE exports through the Yanbu and
Fujairah pipelines continue, with the East-West pipeline back to
normal after the early September attacks. Tanker crossings have
risen, although transponders remain switched off, leaving visible

transit at 1% to 4% of pre-conflict levels, while cargo transfers in
the Gulf of Oman have increased as an alternative route.
However, these workarounds are slower and more costly. Ship-
to-ship transfers can take 5 to 10 days, US escorts through
the Gulf via a route close to the Oman shoreline cause delays
while some tankers are taking longer routes. Fewer ships are
also available, with many redeployed to other markets. Higher
insurance premiums are adding to costs, with Gulf-to-China

tanker rates above US$0.9mn a day, around 15x higher than a
year ago. With prompt barrels (available for physical delivery)
scarce, the gap between physical and futures prices widened
again, with Dated Brent around US$136/bbl in early October
against US$101/bbl for front-month futures.
Oil and fuel prices push higher ...
Refined prices have risen more than crude
Refined product markets were already tight going into the
conflict. Reduced Gulf supply, exacerbated by Ukrainian strikes

on Russian refineries, has added to that tightness. Together
with higher shipping costs and rising Dated Brent prices,
Singapore Gasoil 10ppm (diesel) has roughly doubled since the
Strait closed, compared with around 40% for crude futures.
Consequently, Singapore gasoil crack spread widened
to an average of around US$36.1/bbl in early October,
from US$17.5/bbl across 2025. Alongside the Australian
government’s excise tax returning to normal, after it had been

halved, this has lifted national diesel and petrol prices to
around A$2.83/L and A$2.41/L.
Outlook
We do not see a lasting US-Iran agreement until at least
mid-2027. In the meantime, we assume there will not be a
return to full-scale military intervention. However, periodic
Iranian attacks on ships and facilities are expected to continue,
as will the US blockade of Iranian exports, keeping a supply
risk premium in place. We look for Brent futures to average

around US$101/bbl in Q4, then ease gradually as demand
from refiners and governments to rebuild stocks partly offsets
recovering supply. As the premium fades and the market
moves into surplus in late 2027, we forecast Brent at around
US$77/bbl in Q4 2027, easing towards US$70/bbl in 2028 as
Middle East production fully recovers.
Oil balance to gradually improve over 2027
Global oil supply is expected to rise by 4.0mb/d (4.0%) to
103.7mb/d in 2027, following a 6.1% fall in 2026. Most of

this reflects a gradual recovery in Gulf output, with Houthi
Sian Fenner, Head of Business & Industry Economics
Justin Smirk, Senior Economist
Luka Belobajdic, Economist

### Page 3

are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
attacks on Red Sea shipping keeping Saudi production below
its pre-conflict level until H2 2027. Meanwhile, we look for UAE
production to continue to improve over 2027 and lift above
pre-conflict levels from 2028, as it is no longer constrained by

OPEC quotas. We do not expect a meaningful return of Iranian
oil until an agreement is reached, and damage sustained during
the conflict means production will take time to recover.
We expect global oil demand to be 103.7mb/d in 2026, 0.8mb/d
(0.7%) higher than in 2025, and to rise a further 1.9mb/d (1.8%)
to 105.6mb/d in 2027. Chinese crude imports will continue to
recover over the coming months as refiners restock ahead of
Lunar New Year, but we expect only a gradual return to pre-

conflict levels, as the electrification of transport limits the
recovery in Chinese demand. Globally, demand for diesel and jet
fuel recovers as transport and aviation activity picks up, although
some demand destruction and efficiency gains are expected
to be permanent. The conflict has also highlighted strategic
vulnerabilities, particularly across Asia, so we expect a push to
rebuild reserves adding around 0.5% to demand over 2027.
Fuel prices to ease more slowly than crude

Refined product supply is expected to recover more slowly
than crude, with Gulf refinery runs not returning to pre-war
levels until at least late 2027. Outside the Gulf, there has
been some constructive news. The US has lifted sanctions
on Russian diesel until April 2027, with Russia saying it will
supply more than 4Mt over the coming months, while China
has lifted its ban on refined product exports. However, we
are cautious about how much this will add to the market in

the short term. Much of the Russian supply is headed to the
US and will depend on Russia’s ability to restore refining and
export capacity, while doubts remain over the Russia-Ukraine
energy ceasefire announced by President Trump. China still
manages exports through quotas, which are likely to increase
only gradually as domestic stocks are rebuilt. Refinery runs
should lift ahead of Lunar New Year, but any increase in
exports will take time to reach regional markets.

With product supply recovering slowly and depleted inventories
being rebuilt, Singapore gasoil crack is likely to stay above its
long-run average even as Brent eases, narrowing from US$71/
bbl in Q4 to US$27/bbl by Q4 2027. With Singapore Gas 10ppm
prices, shipping and insurance costs to remain elevated and
easing only gradually in 2027, we forecast Australian diesel
to retail at an average of 274¢/L in Q4, easing to 200¢/L in Q4
2027, and petrol at 230¢/L and 187¢/L respectively.

LNG prices supported through 2027
Japanese LNG prices surged 15.2%mth in September,
averaging US$25.4/MMBtu as supply disruptions in Qatar
and its inability to divert exports around the Strait of Hormuz
tightened markets and intensified competition between
importers. While prices are now more than double pre-conflict
levels, demand destruction across Asia is providing some
offset to tight supply
Our Q4 forecast for Japanese LNG is now US$24.5/mmbtu and

we expect the market to remain structurally tight, with supply
recovery likely to be gradual and prices not returning to pre-
conflict levels until around 2028. Still, Australia’s domestic
gas market should remain insulated, supported by the FY2027
Federal Budget’s domestic gas reservation scheme, which
requires exporters to supply the domestic market with the
equivalent of up to 20% of gas exports. So far this year,
despite the increase in LNG spot prices in Asia, Australian

natural gas prices have remained under the $12 per GJ cap
that has proposed by the Commonwealth government. We are
expecting this to continue well into 2027.
Risks around our oil price outlook are two-sided
Talks are still ongoing in the background, with the US and
Iran exchanging proposals to reopen the Strait, but the gap
between them remains wide. The conflict has also expanded
to the Red Sea and, with inventories sharply drawn down,
there is a smaller buffer to absorb a further shock.

Using the Oxford Economics model, we assess an escalation
scenario, where energy markets face a deeper and more
persistent disruption as the conflict in the Middle East
intensifies. In this case, Brent futures rise to around US$150/
bbl in H1 2027, only dipping to below US$100/bbl in Q2 2028.
We also consider an early resolution scenario, where shipping
and Gulf supply normalise faster, returning Brent to near pre-
conflict levels by end-2027.
Escalation scenario

In the escalation scenario we assume:
Talks stall and the conflict escalates:  the gap between the
US and Iran proves too wide and the US resumes strikes after
the midterms. Iran responds with attacks on shipping and Gulf
energy infrastructure, damaging export, storage and refining
facilities. A lasting agreement is not reached until 2028.
Export routes are disrupted: more frequent attacks curtail
dark crossings of the Strait and ship-to-ship transfers in the

Gulf of Oman. Houthi attacks also cut off Saudi alternative
routes, forcing Gulf producers to cut output and Middle
East crude exports sharply lower. New routes and shipping
arrangements eventually support a recovery in Gulf flows, but
trade patterns take time to adjust.
... and risks remain and while they are two sided ...

are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
4
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Product supply fails to recover:  The Russia–Ukraine energy
ceasefire does not hold and strikes on Russian refineries
continue, limiting how much diesel Russia can export,
even with the US sanctions waiver extended. China also

reintroduces its export ban to protect domestic supply.
Oil prices lift to around US$150/bbl in H1 2027: With little
buffer to absorb the supply loss, competition for physical
delivery intensifies, with physical prices well above futures
as prompt barrels remain scarce. Higher prices support
supply growth outside OPEC and drive demand destruction,
alongside further emergency stock releases, but a deeper
and more prolonged global deficit remains. Brent stays above

US$100/bbl until mid-2028 as governments rebuild reserves.
The Singapore gasoil crack widens and, with freight and
insurance costs also higher, Australian diesel and petrol prices
average around 247 cpl and 231 cpl at end 2027. Japanese
LNG also remains elevated around US$23.2/MMBtu in Q4
2027, $9.1 above our baseline.
Early resolution scenario
In the early resolution scenario we assume:
A durable agreement: The US and Iran agree terms to reopen
the Strait by the end of year, and, unlike the June interim deal,

the agreement holds, with progress towards a permanent
settlement in early 2027. Attacks on shipping cease and the
US blockade is lifted, allowing Iranian oil back into the market,
initially from storage. Vessels are quickly repositioned to the
Gulf, with freight and war-risk insurance easing from early
2027 rather than mid-year.
A faster recovery in supply:  With export routes secure, Gulf
production and damaged refinery and export capacity recover

sooner than in the baseline. Outside the Gulf, the US extends
its waiver on Russian diesel beyond April 2027 and China
raises export quotas as domestic stocks are rebuilt.
Demand settles below pre-conflict trend: Some demand
destruction and efficiency gains persist, particularly in China.
With inventories rebuilding faster, government restocking is
smaller and more gradual than in the baseline.
Prices fall quickly, but restocking provides a floor:  Brent

Brent futures ease to around US$88/bbl in Q1 2027 as the
premium for prompt physical barrels unwinds. As the market
moves into balance, Brent eases to around US$65/bbl by Q4
2027, close to pre-conflict levels.
Refined product prices ease faster on lower freight costs with
the Singapore gasoil crack narrowing. Australian diesel and
petrol prices fall to around 182 cpl and 170 cpl by Q4 2027. Gas
markets also ease, although damage to Qatar’s gas facility means

export capacity recovers gradually. Japanese LNG falls to around
US$11.9/MMBtu by end-2027, $2.1 below our baseline.
Economic impact
In the escalation scenario, higher fuel costs flow through to
transport, food and other energy-intensive goods such as
plastics and fertilisers. Australian headline CPI remains above
4.5% for much of 2027, with underlying inflation remaining
sticky at above 3% until late 2028. The RBA faces a difficult
trade off between much higher inflation and a significant

hit to activity, as households cut discretionary spending and
businesses defer investment. Higher LNG export earnings
provide only a moderate offset. In the early resolution scenario,
headline inflation is around 0.1ppt below the baseline in 2027.
New Zealand is more exposed in both scenarios, with food
and energy a larger share of its CPI and no offsetting energy
exports. Headline inflation is around 1ppt higher in the
escalation scenario in Q1 2027.

... the upside presents a more dramatic shift in prices

Document 399: Q3 Growth in advanced economies_ solid despite shocks (2026-10-23)

Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-23
Pages: 2

### Page 1

Paris, 23 October 2026

EcoFlash

Q3 Growth in advanced economies: solid despite shocks

Growth looks to have held up in Q3 despite successive shocks (see our analysis), as it did in 2025 and H1 2026. A longer 
Middle East conflict has fed into more persistent inflation; drought has worsened in many countries, and interest rates 
have risen further – at the short end, with both the ECB and the Fed raising policy rates in September, and even more at 
the long end. However, our nowcasts show that growth is bending without breaking in most advanced economies. GDP 
figures (due on 29 October in the US and 30 October for the euro area and its member states) are expected to confirm 
that. In the Euro area, momentum looks stable (excluding Ireland, whose GDP is volatile). In Germany, growth has been 
uninterrupted since Q4 2025, while France is likely to have returned to growth after stagnating in H1. In the US, growth 
is likely to have gathered pace, according to the GDP Now, still driven by AI, but increasingly by other sectors as well.

Euro area: resilience beneath the surface. Our nowcast points to growth slowing to 0.3% in q/q, though the pace is stable 
excluding Ireland (Irish GDP is volatile and had markedly boosted the Q2 figure). Excluding Ireland, growth should hold at its H2 
average, a sign of the bloc’s underlying resilience. Also, momentum has broadened to a growing number of sectors ( see our 
analysis). While industrial production stalled over the summer, services remained buoyant. The services PMI rebounded sharply 
in Q3 and sits firmly in expansion territory (53.0 in September). Improving job-related survey data (PMI and European Commission 
surveys) and a stable unemployment rate (6.4% in August) hint at more hiring in Q3 – a support for household consumption that 
partly offsets purchasing-power losses from persistent inflation. Corporate investment is underpinned by buoyant new investment 
lending (8.7% y/y In August, 12-month cumulative). 
France: growing, nevertheless. The succession of shocks does not appear to have prevented France from returning to 
growth in Q3, at 0.2% q/q, according to our nowcast. One-off factors accounted for the stall in activity in H1. In Q3, the drought 
worsened and industrial production remained subdued. However, market services production rebounded in July and August, 
notably in business services, restaurants, digital and real estate. The latter points to residential investment recovering after a weak 
H1. Corporate investment likely followed, as reflected in buoyant credit flows and AI-related investment. Household consumption 
is likely to have improved slightly on higher electric vehicle sales.  
Euro area  France

Source: Refinitiv, BNP Paribas  Source: Refinitiv, BNP Paribas

Germany: growth still solid. The H1 rebound in activity is moderating but continues. Our nowcast points to a fourth straight 
quarter of growth in Q3, at 0.2% q/q (after 0.3% in Q4 2025, 0.4% in Q1 2026, and 0.3% in Q2). Manufacturing activity eased 
slightly, according to the September PMI survey (53.9, down 0.4 m/m). Government spending will remain growth-supportive, with 
defence and infrastructure outlays gaining traction. Infrastructure spending continues to underpin activity in public works, which 
stands at its highest since November 2021 (2.5% 3m/3m in August). Exports (4.4% y/y between January and August) remain the 
main growth engine, helped by a pivot towards Europe (7.2%). 
United States: an expansion undeterred by inflation. Growth is likely to have gathered pace in Q3. The Atlanta Fed’s GDP 
Now points to a 3.5% annualized growth rate (AR), close to our own 3.7% forecast (0.9% non -AR) and up from 2.2% in 
Q2. Domestic demand remains the engine. Consumption is weathering the pickup in inflation, while the AI-driven capex cycle 
remains robust – as reflected in retail sales and nondefence capital-goods orders (excl. aircraft). As a result, growth in final sales

### Page 2

to private domestic purchasers 1 should print above 4% AR again. By contrast, external trade should remain a drag on growth, 
reflecting AI-related import needs. Survey data point to mome ntum that is both solid and broad -based. The ISM indices have 
remained firmly in growth territory (54.9 quarterly average for manufacturing and services alike). At the same time, they highlight 
persistent price and supply-chain pressures.

Germany   United States

Source: Refinitiv, BNP Paribas   Source: Refinitiv, Atlanta Fed, BNP Paribas
